Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis

Respiratory Disease MONDO:0800504 Pathograph 52 Show in embeddings browser Respiratory Disease Lung Disease

A chronic progressive fibrosing interstitial pneumonia of unknown cause, occurring primarily in older adults and associated with the usual interstitial pneumonia (UIP) radiologic and histologic pattern. Alveolar epithelial dysfunction, impaired regeneration, fibroblast activation and persistent matrix remodeling reduce lung compliance and gas exchange. IPF is diagnosed after exclusion of alternative causes of interstitial lung disease; UIP is not specific to IPF. Familial pulmonary fibrosis and telomere biology disorders overlap with IPF but also produce other interstitial lung disease diagnoses.

Inheritance

1
Autosomal dominant predisposition in a molecularly defined subset HP:0000006
Some familial cases have heterozygous pathogenic variants with age-dependent incomplete penetrance and variable ILD phenotype. Most IPF does not follow a single Mendelian pattern. Short telomeres can also be inherited without the familial variant, complicating recurrence prediction.
Autosomal dominant inheritance Penetrance: INCOMPLETE
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/ SUPPORT Other
"Each child of an individual with a ... associated with predisposition to pulmonary fibrosis has a 50% chance of inheriting the pathogenic variant."
The recurrence statement applies to autosomal dominant monogenic predisposition, not every sporadic IPF case; inheritance of a variant does not predict age of onset or guarantee disease.
◈

Mechanistic Hypotheses

3
Injury-Centric Wound Healing Model
injury_centric_model CANONICAL
Evidence balance 1 support
Repetitive alveolar epithelial injury is modeled as the primary upstream driver; AT2 cell senescence and subsequent fibroblast activation are downstream consequences of failed wound healing. Fibrosis represents an aberrant, non-resolving repair response to injury in genetically or environmentally susceptible individuals.
Retained as CANONICAL as a useful injury-and-aberrant-repair framework. Susceptibility, persistent signaling and regenerative failure can sustain fibrosis after an initiating insult; the framework does not require an identified recurrent exposure in every patient.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35563849 SUPPORT Other
"it has been suggested that repeated microinjuries of epithelial cells induce a wound healing response, during which fibroblasts differentiate into myofibroblasts."
Supports the injury-centric model in which repetitive microinjury is the upstream initiating event that leads to fibroblast activation.
Senescence-First (Stem-Cell Exhaustion) Model
senescence_first_model ALTERNATIVE
Evidence balance 9 support
AT2 senescence and loss of regenerative capacity may be proximal drivers of fibrosis, particularly in telomere-associated disease but also after other stress pathways. Human genetics and tissue studies support relevance; TRF1 and Sin3a mouse perturbations demonstrate spontaneous fibrosis, whereas other telomerase models require injury. Immune-cell-free organoids establish one epithelial-fibroblast route. The relative contributions of this route and immune amplification in human disease remain unresolved.
Retain ALTERNATIVE as a hypothesis about causal ordering, without assigning a validated exclusive patient subtype. OpenScientist provides useful multi-trigger and multi-cellular leads, but its universal second-hit and obligate-immune claims are rejected. Short leukocyte telomere frequencies do not define the fraction of IPF mediated by AT2 senescence. TERT deletion, telomere shortening and shelterin uncapping are distinct perturbations. The assessment records corrected source interpretation and missing declared artifacts.

Alveolar epithelial telomere dysfunction -> AT2 progenitor exhaustion and impaired alveolar regeneration INDIRECT KNOWN INTERMEDIATES Dysfunctional telomeres impair alveolar epithelial renewal; this contribution depends on the genetic and injury context.

PMID:37653024 SUPPORT In Vitro
"the autocrine TGF-β-positive feedback loop in AT2-lineage cells is a critical cellular system in non-inflammatory lung fibrogenesis."
Immune-cell-free organoids support an epithelial-fibroblast route in vitro, without proving whole-animal immune independence.
PMID:33808277 SUPPORT Other
"Loss of regenerative potential of alveolar type II epithelial cells (AT2) cells following injury has been postulated to underlie telomeropathy-associated lung fibrosis, with concomitant excessive proliferation of airway cells displaying abnormal phenotypes"
Review describes telomere-driven loss of AT2 regenerative capacity as a mechanistic explanation for the strong age and telomere-length associations in IPF.
PMID:33197388 SUPPORT Computational
"Meta-analysis of the two-sample mendelian randomisation results provided evidence inferring that shorter telomeres cause IPF"
Published Mendelian randomization supports a contribution of shorter telomeres to IPF susceptibility under instrumental-variable assumptions. The COPD comparator does not establish disease specificity across all conditions or AT2-specific causal timing.
+ 6 more references
Convergent Self-Sustaining Feedback Loop Model
feedback_loop_convergence_model ALTERNATIVE
Evidence balance 1 support
Epithelial stress, senescence, fibroblast persistence and matrix-derived mechanical signals may reinforce one another after initiation. Experimental TGF-beta and matrix-stiffness feedback support this convergent framework, but their relative necessity and therapeutic importance in human IPF remain unresolved.
This synthesis complements upstream injury and senescence hypotheses. Response to an antifibrotic drug does not by itself identify the initiating lesion or establish which feedback loop mediated benefit.
Pathograph links
Excessive extracellular matrix deposition -> Fibroblast activation and myofibroblast differentiation INDIRECT KNOWN INTERMEDIATES Matrix mechanical signals can reinforce fibroblast activation; the review supports a feedback framework without quantifying its necessity in humans.
Persistent epithelial and mesenchymal TGF-beta signaling -> AT2 cell senescence and SASP INDIRECT KNOWN INTERMEDIATES Autocrine TGF-beta sustains the profibrotic AT2-lineage state in the tested organoid model.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34813355 SUPPORT Other
"The pathogenesis of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) involves a complex interplay of cell types and signaling pathways. Recurrent alveolar epithelial cell (AEC) injury may occur in the context of predisposing factors (e.g., genetic, environmental, epigenetic, immunologic, and gerontologic),..."
Annual Review of Pathology review frames IPF as a convergence of multiple predisposing factors and cell types, consistent with the feedback loop model in which no single upstream event fully explains the disease.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
Is repetitive alveolar epithelial injury the primary upstream driver of IPF, or does age-related AT2 cell senescence and telomere attrition precede and predispose to fibrosis independently of ongoing injury?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN disc_ipf_injury_vs_senescence_ordering
Attached to
pathophysiology#AT2 cell senescence and SASP
Human disease likely combines epithelial susceptibility, injury and failed repair. TRF1 and Sin3a perturbations demonstrate spontaneous fibrosis in mice, while AT2-specific TERT loss and certain surfactant variants require additional injury. The unresolved question is how these distinct model-specific mechanisms map onto human causal ordering, not whether any spontaneous model exists.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:27699234 SUPPORT Model Organism
"These results demonstrate that prolonged telomere dysfunction in type II AECs, but not collagen-expressing cells, leads to age-dependent lung remodeling and fibrosis."
Conditional TRF1 deletion causes spontaneous fibrosis after prolonged epithelial telomere dysfunction; this severe uncapping model is distinct from ordinary age-related shortening.
PMID:32991815 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We used conditional loss of Sin3a in adult mouse AT2 cells to initiate a program of p53-dependent cellular senescence, AT2 cell depletion, and spontaneous, progressive pulmonary fibrosis."
An AT2-directed genetic perturbation produces spontaneous progressive fibrosis without bleomycin; it does not establish immune independence or the initiating lesion in all human IPF.
Which epithelial, mesenchymal and matrix-derived feedback processes sustain progressive IPF, how necessary are they in different patients, and which combinations are therapeutically tractable?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN disc_ipf_ecm_feedback_irreversibility
Attached to
pathophysiology#Excessive extracellular matrix deposition pathophysiology#Aberrant basaloid cell emergence
Autocrine TGF-beta, matrix mechanics and persistent mesenchymal states can sustain experimental fibrosis. Human relative contributions and optimal combinations of interventions remain unresolved. Antifibrotic therapies slow FVC decline but do not establish reversal, while senolytic clinical studies remain small feasibility trials.
Does gut dysbiosis drive IPF progression (dysbiosis → fibrosis), or is it a secondary consequence of systemic inflammation and drug effects in established IPF (fibrosis → dysbiosis)? Are microbial metabolite deficits (SCFA, secondary bile acids) individually sufficient to amplify pulmonary fibrosis, or do they require cofactors? Can fecal microbiota transplantation attenuate pulmonary inflammation in human ILD?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN disc_ipf_gut_lung_axis_causality
Attached to
pathophysiology#Gut dysbiosis and microbial metabolite dysregulation
The reviewed evidence combines clinical associations and preclinical gut-lung experiments. Directionality, specific microbial mediators, and independence from disease severity, diet and drug exposure remain unresolved. Human microbiome-directed therapeutic benefit is not established by the cited review.
Proposed experiments
Longitudinal microbiome profiling in IPF cohorts
exp_ipf_microbiome_temporal_cohort
Use longitudinal microbiome profiling with matched lung-function measures, diet, medication and severity covariates to assess temporal association. Temporal precedence alone does not establish causality.
Germ-free and antibiotic-depleted mouse IPF models
exp_ipf_germ_free_bleomycin
Germ-free or antibiotic-depleted mouse bleomycin models to test whether microbiota depletion attenuates pulmonary fibrosis, establishing a causal contribution of gut microbiota to lung fibrotic pathways.
SCFA supplementation in IPF mouse models
exp_ipf_scfa_supplementation
SCFA (butyrate and propionate) supplementation in bleomycin-induced pulmonary fibrosis mouse models to determine whether restoring microbial metabolite levels reduces fibrotic burden and pro-inflammatory cytokines.

Pathophysiology

20
Repetitive alveolar epithelial injury and aberrant repair
Recurrent injury to alveolar epithelial cells, particularly type II pneumocytes, initiates a dysregulated wound-healing response that fails to restore normal alveolar architecture and instead drives fibrotic remodeling.

PMID:35563849 SUPPORT Other
"it has been suggested that repeated microinjuries of epithelial cells induce a wound healing response, during which fibroblasts differentiate into myofibroblasts."
This review directly supports repetitive epithelial injury as the initiating trigger for maladaptive wound healing in IPF.
PMID:33201251 SUPPORT Other
"Apoptosis, senescence, epithelial-mesenchymal transition, endothelial-mesenchymal transition, and epithelial cell migration have been shown to play a key role in IPF-associated tissue remodeling."
This review links epithelial apoptosis and senescence to the abnormal remodeling response after lung injury in IPF.
AT2 cell senescence and SASP
AT2-lineage cells can enter a stress-associated senescent state with reduced regenerative capacity and a secretory program that activates fibroblasts. DNA damage, telomere dysfunction, chronic Wnt signaling and metabolic stress are candidate upstream routes. Bleomycin-treated epithelial-fibroblast organoids show autocrine TGF-beta feedback and paracrine myofibroblast induction without immune cells. In vivo models establish senescence as a possible proximal driver, while the contribution of immune recruitment varies by model and cannot be declared universally necessary or dispensable.

PMID:37653024 SUPPORT In Vitro
"bleomycin causes DNA damage and activates p53 signaling in AT2-lineage cells, leading to AT2-to-AT1 transition-like state with a senescence-associated secretory phenotype (SASP). Among SASP-related factors, TGF-β plays an exclusive role in promoting lung fibroblast-to-myofibroblast..."
The immune-cell-free organoid experiment establishes epithelial-fibroblast sufficiency under bleomycin stimulation; accompanying mouse genetics do not imply immune independence of whole-animal disease.
PMID:34813355 SUPPORT Other
"Recurrent alveolar epithelial cell (AEC) injury may occur in the context of predisposing factors (e.g., genetic, environmental, epigenetic, immunologic, and gerontologic), leading to metabolic dysfunction, senescence, aberrant epithelial cell activation, and dysregulated epithelial repair."
Annual Review of Pathology 2022 review places AT2 senescence as a consequence of recurrent injury that bridges injury to downstream fibrotic mechanisms.
PMID:32991815 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We used conditional loss of Sin3a in adult mouse AT2 cells to initiate a program of p53-dependent cellular senescence, AT2 cell depletion, and spontaneous, progressive pulmonary fibrosis."
An AT2-directed genetic perturbation produces spontaneous progressive fibrosis without bleomycin; it does not establish immune independence or the initiating lesion in all human IPF.
Alveolar epithelial telomere dysfunction
Pathogenic telomere-maintenance variants and age-related shortening can produce dysfunctional telomeres and persistent DNA-damage signaling in alveolar epithelial cells. The human genetic evidence supports susceptibility, with incomplete penetrance. Experimental shelterin/TRF1 uncapping can induce spontaneous lung fibrosis, whereas AT2-specific TERT loss increases bleomycin susceptibility without measurable telomere shortening in one model. These perturbations must not be treated as interchangeable demonstrations of physiological telomere attrition.

Human pedigrees link rare variants to telomere dysfunction and familial pulmonary fibrosis.
AT2 progenitor exhaustion and impaired alveolar regeneration
AT2 cells normally regenerate the alveolar epithelium. Senescence and telomere dysfunction impair renewal and can deplete functional progenitors. Cell loss and a persistent senescent secretory state are distinct mechanisms: the Sin3a model distinguishes progressive senescence-associated fibrosis from transient remodeling after AT2 ablation.

Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:18753630 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Short telomeres limit tissue renewal capacity in the lung and germ-line mutations in telomerase components, hTERT and hTR, underlie inheritance in a subset of families with IPF."
Establishes that short telomeres limit lung tissue-renewal capacity, supporting exhaustion of the alveolar type II progenitor pool. Evidence source is HUMAN_CLINICAL (patient telomere-length study).
PMID:32991815 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We establish that senescence rather than loss of AT2 cells promotes progressive fibrosis and show that either genetic or pharmacologic interventions targeting p53 activation or senescence block fibrogenesis."
Senescence-associated dysfunction cannot be reduced to simple progenitor depletion.
Aberrant basaloid cell emergence
KRT5-negative/KRT17-positive aberrant basaloid epithelial cells coexpress basal, mesenchymal and senescence-related markers and occur near myofibroblast foci in fibrotic human lungs. Their transcriptome is a disease-associated state rather than proof of epithelial conversion into fibroblasts. Human AT2 cultures can undergo a basal transition under defined signaling conditions, but the origin and causal contribution of every basaloid cell population in human IPF remain unresolved.

Gut dysbiosis and microbial metabolite dysregulation
Altered gut microbial composition and metabolites have been associated with interstitial lung disease, and preclinical models suggest effects on immune responses and fibrosis. Directionality, specific mediators and independence from diet, disease severity and antifibrotic treatment remain unresolved in human IPF. This is an emerging modifier rather than an established universal upstream mechanism.

Review-level evidence summarizes clinical associations and preclinical gut-lung mechanisms; it does not establish the specified causal sequence or direction in human IPF.
Profibrotic macrophage recruitment and amplification
Recruited monocyte-derived macrophages can amplify fibrosis after epithelial injury through CCR2-dependent pathways. Macrophage behavior changes with cell state and disease phase: some macrophage-derived signals promote resolution and fibroblast apoptosis. Neither a uniformly profibrotic macrophage role nor universal immune dependence follows from these models.

PMID:31922885 SUPPORT Model Organism
"In contrast, mice with lung epithelial cell-specific deletion of CCL12 were protected from bleomycin-induced fibrosis and had expression of CCL2 and CCL7 similar to that of control mice treated with bleomycin. Deletion of CCL12 within AECs led to decreased recruitment of exudate macrophages."
Epithelial CCL12 contributes to macrophage recruitment in this mouse injury model; global CCL12 deletion was not protective and this is not universal immune necessity.

Macrophage-dependent effects can also favor resolution in the SIRT3 restoration model.
IL-11 Autocrine Signalling in Lung Fibroblasts
IL-11 is increased in human IPF lungs and secreted by IPF fibroblasts. In cultured lung fibroblasts it promotes ERK-dependent, posttranscriptional myofibroblast conversion. Neutralizing IL-11 reverses fibrosis in a mouse model; efficacy in human IPF is not established by that experiment.

Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:31554736 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"interleukin-11 (IL11) is up-regulated in the lung of patients with IPF, associated with disease severity, and IL-11 is secreted from IPF fibroblasts"
Establishes IL-11 up-regulation in patient IPF lung and its secretion by IPF fibroblasts, grounding this node in human disease. Evidence source is HUMAN_CLINICAL (patient lung tissue).
PMID:31554736 SUPPORT In Vitro
"IL-11 stimulates lung fibroblasts to become invasive actin alpha 2, smooth muscle-positive (ACTA2+), collagen-secreting myofibroblasts in an extracellular signal-regulated kinase (ERK)-dependent, posttranscriptional manner"
Directly evidences the IL-11 to ERK receptor-signalling arm driving fibroblast-to-myofibroblast conversion modeled by this node. Evidence source is IN_VITRO (cultured lung fibroblasts).
PMID:31554736 SUPPORT Model Organism
"anti-IL-11 treatment diminished lung inflammation and reversed lung fibrosis while inhibiting ERK and SMAD activation in mice"
Shows pharmacological IL-11 inhibition reverses lung fibrosis and lowers ERK activation in vivo, validating the drug-target link on this node. Evidence source is MODEL_ORGANISM (bleomycin mouse model).
Fibroblast activation and myofibroblast differentiation
Resident mesenchymal cells become matrix-producing myofibroblasts in response to profibrotic mediators including TGF-beta and IL-11 and mechanical signals from matrix. Epithelial plasticity can alter the signaling environment, but coexpression of mesenchymal markers does not establish that epithelial cells are the source of pathogenic fibroblasts.

Microengineered alveolar array lung-on-chip with breathing mechanics ORGAN ON CHIP Cyclic stretch applied together with TGF-beta 1 amplified fibrotic signalling well beyond TGF-beta 1 alone. This makes breathing motion an explicit cofactor of fibroblast activation rather than background, which is the mechanistic claim a static culture cannot test.

This review directly supports fibroblast-to-myofibroblast differentiation as a core transition downstream of epithelial injury.
PMID:32549377 SUPPORT Other
"TGF-β is a critical cytokine that drives development of fibrosis."
This review supports TGF-beta signaling as a central profibrotic pathway in IPF.
Excessive extracellular matrix deposition
Persistent matrix-producing mesenchymal cells deposit collagen and other extracellular matrix components. Progressive scarring stiffens and distorts the lung. Matrix-derived mechanical signals can reinforce fibroblast activation, without proving an irreversible autonomous scar state in every patient.

PMID:32549377 SUPPORT Other
"changes in gene expression, disrupted glycolysis, and mitochondrial oxidation, dysregulated protein folding, and altered phospholipid and sphingolipid metabolism result in activation of myofibroblast, deposition of extracellular matrix proteins, remodeling of lung architecture and fibrosis."
This review links activated myofibroblasts directly to ECM deposition, architectural remodeling, and fibrosis in IPF.
PMID:38232990 SUPPORT Other
"Different cell types (epithelial cells, endothelial cells, fibroblasts and macrophages) interact dynamically through multiple signalling pathways, including biochemical/molecular and mechanical signals, such as stiffness, affecting cell function and differentiation."
Supports the mechano-transduction concept: matrix stiffness signals through integrin pathways to maintain fibroblast activation independently of soluble TGF-beta, providing a mechanistic basis for the feedback edge from ECM deposition back to fibroblast activation.
Architectural distortion and gas-exchange failure
Progressive interstitial scarring distorts alveolar units, decreases lung compliance and increases elastic recoil. Restricted lung expansion, loss of functioning alveolar-capillary surface and impaired oxygen transfer produce exertional limitation, hypoxemia and ultimately respiratory failure.
lung UBERON:0002048 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in lung (UBERON:0002048). UBERON:0002048 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Dyspnea Reduced compliance and impaired gas exchange increase the burden of exertion and breathlessness.
Exercise intolerance Fibrotic physiologic impairment limits exercise capacity.
Hypoxemia Disrupted alveolar-capillary gas exchange impairs oxygenation.
Respiratory failure Advanced architectural destruction produces severe gas-exchange failure.
Hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction and vascular remodeling Impaired ventilation and hypoxic alveolar regions can trigger compensatory vasoconstriction with maladaptive consequences when persistent.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32549377 SUPPORT Other
"The compromised architecture leads to disturbed gas exchange, decreased lung compliance, and respiratory failure and death."
This review directly supports structural distortion as the mechanism linking fibrosis to respiratory failure and mortality.
Distal MUC5B overexpression and impaired clearance
The common MUC5B promoter risk allele increases pulmonary expression. Distal-airspace MUC5B overexpression impairs clearance and worsens persistent fibrosis after bleomycin in mice. This supplies a genetic susceptibility route distinct from telomere dysfunction; interaction with short leukocyte telomeres has been studied epidemiologically but its cellular mechanism remains unresolved.

PMID:21506741 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A common polymorphism in the promoter of MUC5B is associated with familial interstitial pneumonia and idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis."
This human case-control study identifies the MUC5B promoter variant rs35705950 as a major inherited susceptibility factor for IPF.
PMID:30560893 SUPPORT Model Organism
"In mice, we demonstrate that Muc5b concentration in bronchoalveolar epithelia is related to impaired mucociliary clearance (MCC) and to the extent and persistence of bleomycin-induced lung fibrosis."
Muc5b overexpression is a susceptibility amplifier in an injury model, not proof that this allele alone initiates fibrosis in humans.
Surfactant dysfunction and epithelial proteostasis stress
Rare surfactant variants predispose a subset of familial interstitial lung disease, sometimes classified as IPF. Misprocessing and endoplasmic-reticulum stress can compromise AT2 function, but variants differ: SP-A1 R219W-driven TGF-beta secretion is UPR dependent, whereas SP-C I73T and M71V secretion is largely UPR independent. L188Q SFTPC-induced ER stress increases bleomycin susceptibility without spontaneous fibrosis in one mouse model. Herpesvirus colocalization with UPR markers in human tissue does not establish viral causation.

Chronic canonical Wnt activation
Persistent canonical WNT/beta-catenin activation can shift AT2 cells toward senescence and impaired progenitor behavior in cultures and precision-cut lung slices. Acute and chronic stimulation differ. WNT5A did not reproduce the canonical senescence response in the cited assay; this does not exclude other profibrotic WNT5A actions.

PMID:32109549 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Chronic canonical WNT/β-catenin activation for up to 7 days in primary ATII cells as well as alveolar epithelial cell lines induced a robust cellular senescence, whereas the non-canonical ligand WNT5A was not able to induce cellular senescence."
Sustained canonical signaling induces senescence in these cultures; telomere independence was not directly tested.
AT2 fatty-acid oxidation impairment
FAO gene expression is reduced in human IPF alveolar epithelium. Experimental CPT1a deficiency produces mitochondrial dysfunction, lower SMAD7 and increased TGF-beta signaling, abnormal epithelial intermediates and increased injury susceptibility. These findings support a metabolic contribution to abnormal repair without proving that FAO loss is the initiating event in human IPF.

PMID:39927460 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Furthermore, mice with deficiency of CPT1a in AT2 cells show enhanced susceptibility to developing lung fibrosis with an accumulation of epithelial cells expressing markers of intermediate cells, airway secretory cells, and senescence."
AT2-specific metabolic deficiency amplifies injury-associated fibrosis; it is not an established monogenic cause of human IPF.
Self-DNA sensing through epithelial cGAS
Mitochondrial DNA accumulation in cytosol and extracellular medium accompanies stressed IPF epithelial cultures. cGAS inhibition, RNA interference and DNase experiments support self-DNA-dependent senescence amplification. The study does not demonstrate obligatory STING activation or a requirement for recruited immune cells in every fibrosis model.

Persistent epithelial and mesenchymal TGF-beta signaling
Epithelial and mesenchymal TGF-beta signaling promotes myofibroblast differentiation and dysplastic epithelial repair. In a bleomycin-exposed organoid system an epithelial autocrine loop sustains a profibrotic secretory state. Sustained signaling also redirects human AT2 repair toward basaloid intermediates; this is distinct from epithelial conversion into fibroblasts.

"Among SASP-related factors, TGF-β plays an exclusive role in promoting lung fibroblast-to-myofibroblast differentiation."

PMID:37653024 SUPPORT In Vitro

Perturbations in immune-cell-free epithelial-fibroblast organoids support a local autocrine loop.
Senescent myofibroblast persistence and apoptosis resistance
Aging-associated myofibroblasts can fail to dedifferentiate and become resistant to apoptosis. NOX4/NRF2 redox imbalance, sustained MyoD and BCL-2-dependent survival support persistence in experimental systems. Human IPF fibroblasts share aspects of these states. Senescence therefore contributes through mesenchymal persistence as well as epithelial dysfunction.

PMID:41764163 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Here, we show that prevention of PDGFRα+ fibroblast apoptosis by conditional BCL-2 expression leads to the emergence and persistence of senescent, pro-fibrotic fibroblasts along with enduring, pathologic fibrotic lung remodeling."
Conditional anti-apoptotic signaling demonstrates a fibroblast persistence route in mice, not universal BCL-2 dependence in humans.

"We demonstrated that nonsenescent lung myofibroblasts maintained the capacity for dedifferentiation, whereas senescent/IPF myofibroblasts exhibited an impaired capacity for dedifferentiation."
Cultured IPF fibroblasts show impaired fate reversal.
PMID:24718857 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Nox4 mediated senescence and apoptosis resistance in IPF fibroblasts."
Experimental fibroblast perturbations support redox-dependent persistence.
Age-associated endothelial repair failure
Endothelial ERG dysregulation and capillary cell-state changes accompany aging and fibrotic repair. Conditional endothelial ERG loss impairs fibrosis resolution in mice and enhances paracrine fibroblast activation in culture. Human transcriptional overlap supports relevance but does not establish an independent initiating vascular lesion in all IPF.

Conditional endothelial ERG loss impairs fibrosis resolution in mice; the separate in-vitro edge quotes the fibroblast-activation finding.
Hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction and vascular remodeling
Disturbed ventilation-perfusion and alveolar hypoxia can provoke pulmonary vasoconstriction. Persistent vasoconstriction and vascular remodeling contribute to pulmonary hypertension, alongside cytokine and growth-factor effects. Pulmonary hypertension severity is not determined solely by the extent of fibrosis.

Histopathology

1
Usual interstitial pneumonia pattern
Patchy interstitial fibrosis with temporal and spatial heterogeneity, fibroblast foci and honeycomb remodeling, most marked subpleurally and paraseptally. UIP must be interpreted with clinical and radiologic findings because it is not specific to IPF.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"The histopathologic hallmark and chief diagnostic criterion is a heterogeneous appearance at low magnification in which areas of fibrosis with scarring and honeycomb change alternate with areas of less affected or normal parenchyma"
The statement describes the heterogeneous UIP pattern.
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"The fibrotic zones are composed mainly of dense collagen, although scattered convex subepithelial foci of proliferating fibroblasts and myofibroblasts (so-called fibroblast foci) are a consistent finding."
Fibroblast foci connect the morphology to matrix-producing mesenchymal cells.

PMID:28099038 SUPPORT Human Clinical

SFTPC
Gene: SFTPC hgnc:10802 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is SFTPC (hgnc:10802). hgnc:10802 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Autosomal dominant inheritance with variable expression
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:11991887 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We show that an SFTPC mutation segregates with the pulmonary fibrosis phenotype in this kindred"
Segregation of a heterozygous L188Q variant with familial fibrosis supports a pathogenic SFTPC relationship in a defined kindred; affected adults had UIP and affected children had cellular NSIP. This does not make SFTPC a common cause of sporadic IPF.
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf SUPPORT Other
"Pathogenic variants in the surfactant metabolism genes (SFTPA1, SFTPA2, SFTPC) are typically missense variants that lead to increased endoplasmic reticulum stress."
GeneReviews identifies pathogenic SFTPC variants as a cause of surfactant-related fibrosis predisposition; the precise mechanism depends on the variant.
SFTPA2
Gene: SFTPA2 hgnc:10799 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is SFTPA2 (hgnc:10799). hgnc:10799 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE

Show evidence (2 references)

"These data are consistent with SFTPA2 germline mutations that interfere with protein trafficking and cause familial IPF and lung cancer."
Rare pathogenic variants cause familial fibrosis with lung adenocarcinoma susceptibility; this is a specific genetic subset, not a universal IPF mechanism.
PMID:40491472 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Specifically, heterozygous germline pathogenic or likely pathogenic variants (PGVs) in SFTPA1 and SFTPA2, which encode surfactant proteins SP-A1 and SP-A2, can result in a range of outcomes from asymptomatic cases to severe lung fibrosis and adenocarcinoma, with high mortality risks"
The pedigree study supports age-dependent variable disease expression. Pooled gene estimates, missing exposure data and competing-risk limitations prevent treating its penetrance numbers as universal IPF risks.

Medical Actions

9
Pirfenidone
Action: targeted therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is targeted therapy (NCIT:C93352). NCIT:C93352 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Targeted Therapy NCIT:C93352
Agent: pirfenidone CHEBI:32016 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses pirfenidone (CHEBI:32016). CHEBI:32016 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Oral antifibrotic therapy that slows clinical progression and FVC decline. It is not established to reverse existing scarring. Gastrointestinal and skin adverse effects and liver monitoring affect use.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Fibroblast activation and myofibroblast differentiation — Attenuates TGF-beta-stimulated fibroblast activation in culture; clinical slowing of progression is independently supported by ASCEND.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24613900 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Pirfenidone reduced fibroblast proliferation and attenuated TGF-β-induced α-smooth muscle actin (SMA) and pro-collagen (Col)-I mRNA and protein levels."
Primary human lung fibroblast experiments support an antifibrotic cellular effect; the direct molecular target and mediation of human benefit are not established.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24836312 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Pirfenidone, as compared with placebo, reduced disease progression, as reflected by lung function, exercise tolerance, and progression-free survival, in patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis."
The ASCEND phase 3 trial shows pirfenidone slows clinical progression in IPF.
Nintedanib
Action: targeted therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is targeted therapy (NCIT:C93352). NCIT:C93352 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Targeted Therapy NCIT:C93352
Agent: nintedanib CHEBI:85164 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses nintedanib (CHEBI:85164). CHEBI:85164 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Antifibrotic tyrosine kinase inhibitor that slows the rate of forced vital capacity decline in IPF.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Fibroblast activation and myofibroblast differentiation — Inhibits multiple receptor tyrosine kinases and fibroblast responses; slowing FVC decline does not isolate a single causal kinase pathway.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25745043 SUPPORT Other
"Data from in vitro studies have shown that nintedanib interferes with processes active in fibrosis such as fibroblast proliferation, migration and differentiation, and the secretion of ECM."
The pharmacology review summarizes cellular mechanism evidence separately from randomized clinical efficacy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24836310 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis, nintedanib reduced the decline in FVC, which is consistent with a slowing of disease progression"
The INPULSIS phase 3 trials show nintedanib slows lung function decline in IPF.
Dasatinib and Quercetin (Senolytic Combination)
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: dasatinib CHEBI:49375 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses dasatinib, annotated with dasatinib (anhydrous) (CHEBI:49375). CHEBI:49375 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. quercetin CHEBI:16243 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses quercetin (CHEBI:16243). CHEBI:16243 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Investigational senolytic combination. An uncontrolled 14-participant study suggested physical-function changes without improved pulmonary function. A 12-participant randomized pilot demonstrated feasibility, with more nonserious adverse events on D+Q and no meaningful between-group functional difference. Neither study establishes antifibrotic efficacy or IPF-lung senescent-cell target engagement.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Senescent myofibroblast persistence and apoptosis resistance — Targets senescent fibroblast survival in vitro; efficacy in patients remains investigational.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28230051 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We show that the secretome of senescent fibroblasts, which are selectively killed by a senolytic cocktail, dasatinib plus quercetin (DQ), is fibrogenic."
Senolysis and a profibrotic secretome are demonstrated in fibroblasts; human IPF-lung target engagement remains unproven.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36857968 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Frailty, pulmonary, or physical function were explored before and after intermittent D + Q; though under-powered to evaluate change, these measures do not appear to differ meaningfully between groups."
The randomized pilot supports feasibility, not clinical efficacy.
PMID:36857968 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sleep disturbances and anxiety were disproportionately represented in the D + Q arm (4/6 vs 0/6)."
Adverse-event imbalance prevents an unqualified tolerability claim.
Nerandomilast
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Nerandomilast NCIT:C203229 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Nerandomilast (NCIT:C203229). NCIT:C203229 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Oral preferential PDE4B inhibitor approved for adult IPF. The phase III FIBRONEER-IPF trial showed less FVC decline at 52 weeks, with most participants receiving background nintedanib or pirfenidone. Diarrhea was common. Longer follow-up did not establish a reduction in the composite of acute exacerbation, respiratory hospitalization or death, so survival benefit is not asserted.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Fibroblast activation and myofibroblast differentiation — Preferential PDE4B inhibition suppresses myofibroblast transformation and proliferation in primary IPF fibroblast cultures.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35517783 SUPPORT In Vitro
"BI 1015550 inhibited transforming growth factor-β-stimulated myofibroblast transformation and the mRNA expression of various extracellular matrix proteins, as well as basic fibroblast growth factor plus interleukin-1β-induced cell proliferation."
Primary IPF fibroblast experiments support antiproliferative and differentiation effects; the 2023 corrigendum corrects Table 1 sample sizes without changing this conclusion.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40387033 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis, treatment with nerandomilast resulted in a smaller decline in the FVC than placebo over a period of 52 weeks."
FIBRONEER-IPF randomized 1177 participants, most on background antifibrotics; the endpoint supports slowing decline rather than reversal.
PMID:41738262 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"nerandomilast had no effect on the composite endpoint of time to first acute exacerbation of IPF, hospitalization for respiratory cause, or death"
The final follow-up did not show benefit on this composite clinical endpoint.

url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/ SUPPORT Other
"Adjustment of immunosuppressive medication may be required to avoid drug toxicities in those w/hematologic abnormalities."
Management guidance identifies a telomere-related transplant toxicity consideration rather than a blanket contraindication.
Avoid prednisone-azathioprine-N-acetylcysteine combination
This chronic three-drug regimen should not be used to treat IPF. PANTHER-IPF found excess deaths and hospitalizations without physiological or clinical benefit. This result is specific to the regimen and does not establish that immune cells have no mechanistic role or address every acute-exacerbation treatment question.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22607134 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Increased risks of death and hospitalization were observed in patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis who were treated with a combination of prednisone, azathioprine, and NAC, as compared with placebo."
The randomized PANTHER-IPF result supports avoiding this regimen.
Genetic counseling for inherited fibrosis
Offer specialist counseling when inherited pulmonary fibrosis is suspected or a pathogenic variant is identified. Discuss variable penetrance, possible anticipation and telomere inheritance, family evaluation and the distinction between variant transmission and disease prediction.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/ SUPPORT Other
"Each child of an individual with a ... associated with predisposition to pulmonary fibrosis has a 50% chance of inheriting the pathogenic variant."
The recurrence statement applies to autosomal dominant monogenic predisposition, not every sporadic IPF case; inheritance of a variant does not predict age of onset or guarantee disease.

Environmental Factors

5
Tobacco smoking
Tobacco smoking ECTO:6000029 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is Tobacco smoking, annotated with exposure to tobacco smoking (ECTO:6000029). ECTO:6000029 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Current and former smoking are associated with IPF susceptibility in independent cohorts, with dose-response evidence. A Korean claims cohort identified 25,113 incident cases among 23,242,836 participants; smoking intensity, duration and diagnostic ascertainment affect estimates. Susceptibility is not equivalent to an identified cause in every patient.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34593614 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The risk of IPF was significantly higher in current and former smokers than in never smokers, with an aHR of 1.66 (95% CI 1.61 to 1.72) and 1.42 (95% CI 1.37 to 1.48), respectively."
Korean nationwide claims cohort supports temporality and an exposure association; diagnosis was based on codes and claims.
PMID:32660982 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Ever smoking tobacco was associated with increased risk of IPF: OR 2.20 (95% CI 1.74 to 2.79)"
Independent Australian case-control study included 503 cases and 902 population controls.
Mechanism Target:
PREDISPOSES Repetitive alveolar epithelial injury and aberrant repair — Exposure-associated susceptibility is placed upstream of abnormal alveolar repair. The epidemiologic studies do not isolate the cellular mediators, establish sufficiency, or show that exposure removal reverses established IPF.
Show evidence (2 references)

Occupational respirable and metal dust
Occupational respirable and metal dust ECTO:7000001 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is Occupational respirable and metal dust, annotated with exposure to dust (ECTO:7000001). ECTO:7000001 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Occupational dust exposure is a susceptibility modifier, with inconsistent findings for particular materials. A 78-case Korean study associated metal dust with IPF, whereas an Australian 503-case study supported respirable dust overall but not specific organic, mineral or metal categories. Job-exposure matrices, coexposures and diagnostic differences limit mechanistic inference.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:28157473 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"After adjustment for environmental and military exposures and smoking history, the OR for metal dust exposure was 4.97 (95%CI 1.36-18.17) in multiple logistic regression analysis."
Korean matched case-control study included 78 cases and 78 controls; the interval is wide.
PMID:32660982 REFUTE Human Clinical
"However occupational exposures to other specific organic, mineral or metal dusts were not associated with IPF."
The larger Australian study did not replicate associations with these specific dust categories.
PMID:32660982 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"respirable dust (OR 1.38; 1.04 to 1.82) and asbestos (OR 1.57; 1.15 to 2.15) were independently associated with increased risk of IPF."
Respirable-dust association was observed even though specific metal-dust categories were null.

Occupational wood dust
Occupational wood dust ECTO:7000135 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is Occupational wood dust, annotated with exposure to wood dust (ECTO:7000135). ECTO:7000135 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Pooled observational studies associate wood-dust exposure with IPF, but smoking-adjusted estimates in an earlier meta-analysis included the null. Hardwood, softwood and job-related coexposures are not interchangeable. This is an uncertain susceptibility association rather than a sufficient cause.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38096107 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"ORs and PAFs, respectively, were found to be 1.6 and 7% for metal dust, 1.6 and 3% for wood dust, 1.8 and 14% for agricultural dust, and 1.8 and 4% for silica dust."
Meta-analysis reports pooled associations; attributable fractions rely on causal assumptions and are not demonstrated prevention effects.

Smoking-adjusted analysis in an earlier meta-analysis was compatible with no wood-dust association, limiting certainty.

Occupational asbestos exposure

An Australian IPF case-control study reported an asbestos-exposure association. Biochemical Markers

4
Forced Vital Capacity (Often decreased with restrictive disease; treatment benefit is commonly a slower decline rather than restoration to normal.)
Context: Pulmonary function test readout used to measure restrictive ventilatory impairment and treatment response in pulmonary fibrosis.
Pathograph Readouts
Pharmacodynamic Marker Of Architectural distortion and gas-exchange failure Negative Pharmacodynamic
Higher or less-declining FVC indicates less restrictive physiologic impairment from fibrotic architectural distortion; treatment-induced slowing of FVC decline reports pharmacodynamic slowing of disease progression.
FDA-SE-adult-noncancer-098 FDA
Forced vital capacity (FVC)
Traditional Validated Surrogate Endpoint
Patients with pulmonary fibrosis
Show evidence (1 reference)

"In patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis, nintedanib reduced the decline in FVC, which is consistent with a slowing of disease progression;"
The INPULSIS trials link treatment-induced slowing of FVC decline to slowed IPF disease progression.

"The primary end point was the annual rate of decline in forced vital capacity (FVC)."
The pivotal IPF nintedanib trials used annual FVC decline as the primary physiologic endpoint.

Context: Candidate marker of epithelial stress and disease severity; not an established treatment-selection test.
Pathograph Readouts
Correlates With Architectural distortion and gas-exchange failure Positive Prognostic
Circulating GDF15 associates with severity and survival, while epithelial stress is a proposed biological source. Association does not establish causal mediation.

"circulating concentrations of GDF15 are markedly elevated and correlate with disease severity and survival in multiple independent cohorts."
Human cohorts support a stress-associated prognostic biomarker; this does not prove a profibrotic causal action or IPF-specific senescence burden assay.

Serum KL-6 (INCREASED)
Context: Regenerating type II pneumocytes produce the MUC1-associated KL-6 antigen. Elevated serum levels can correlate with prognosis but are nonspecific; they neither establish IPF alone nor prove a validated surrogate for treatment benefit.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"Serum levels of KL-6 have been shown to be elevated in patients with IPF, and these levels may correlate with increased risk of subsequent mortality"
The guideline summarizes observational serum associations, not a disease-specific diagnostic cutoff or a validated therapeutic surrogate.
PMID:30168753 SUPPORT Other
"Additional recommendations included a conditional recommendation for multidisciplinary discussion and a strong recommendation against measurement of serum biomarkers for the sole purpose of distinguishing IPF from other ILDs."
The diagnostic guideline prevents using a nonspecific serum biomarker alone to distinguish IPF from other interstitial lung diseases.
MMP-7 in plasma and bronchoalveolar lavage (INCREASED)
Context: MMP-7 abundance is associated with IPF and may correlate with disease severity. Circulating or lavage measurements do not directly identify the cellular source, demonstrate a required causal protease mechanism or establish response-surrogate validity.
Show evidence (2 references)

The guideline supports the biochemical association while preserving uncertainty about clinical use and mechanism.

Diagnosis

5
Lung biopsy for histopathologic pattern
Tissue sampling is considered when clinical and imaging assessment cannot establish a sufficiently confident diagnosis and procedural risk is acceptable. Transbronchial cryobiopsy is a conditional alternative to surgical biopsy in centers with appropriate expertise; biopsy is not mandatory for every patient without definite HRCT honeycombing.
biopsy of lung NCIT:C51748 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Results: Histopathologic pattern used to establish or exclude usual interstitial pneumonia.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35486072 SUPPORT Other
"A conditional recommendation was made to regard transbronchial lung cryobiopsy as an acceptable alternative to surgical lung biopsy in centers with appropriate expertise."
The ATS/ERS/JRS/ALAT guideline conditionally recommends transbronchial lung cryobiopsy as an acceptable alternative to surgical lung biopsy where expertise exists.
High-resolution chest CT
Evaluate subpleural and basal reticulation, traction bronchiectasis and honeycombing, together with features suggesting an alternative diagnosis. Integrate the imaging pattern with clinical context.
High Resolution Computed Tomography NCIT:C20644 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"UIP is characterized on HRCT by the presence of reticular opacities, often associated with traction bronchiectasis"
The guideline describes a key radiologic UIP pattern.
Multidisciplinary diagnosis and exclusion of other ILD causes
Integrate symptoms, exposure and medication history, connective-tissue-disease evaluation, HRCT and pathology when available. A UIP pattern alone is not sufficient to identify an idiopathic cause.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"Exclusion of other known causes of ILD (e.g., domestic and occupational environmental exposures, connective tissue disease, and drug toxicity)."
Alternative etiologies must be excluded before assigning IPF.
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"Thus, the accuracy of diagnosis of IPF increases with clinical, radiologic, and histopathologic correlation and can be accomplished with a multidisciplinary discussion among experienced clinical experts in the field of ILDs"
Multidisciplinary integration supports diagnostic accuracy.
Pulmonary function and oxygenation assessment
Assess FVC, diffusion capacity and oxygenation at rest and exertion to characterize impairment and follow progression. These measurements do not identify the etiology of fibrosis by themselves.
Pulmonary Function Test NCIT:C38081 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24836310 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The primary end point was the annual rate of decline in forced vital capacity (FVC)."
The pivotal IPF nintedanib trials used annual FVC decline as the primary physiologic endpoint.
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"The recommendation for long-term oxygen therapy in patients with IPF and clinically significant resting hypoxemia is strong"
The guideline recognizes clinically significant hypoxemia and its treatment.
Genetic and telomere evaluation in at-risk patients
Family history, young onset, premature graying, cytopenias or liver disease can suggest inherited fibrosis or a telomere biology disorder. Consider specialist genetic evaluation and telomere testing in the appropriate context; blood telomere length is not a direct AT2 senescence measurement or a stand-alone IPF diagnosis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/ SUPPORT Other
"Telomere length measurement should be offered to individuals with pulmonary fibrosis and extrapulmonary manifestations of a short telomere syndrome"
The chapter recommends targeted telomere assessment in patients with syndromic clues; this does not require universal testing or establish IPF solely from a short result.
📈

Progression

2
Progressive fibrotic decline
Course is heterogeneous: gradual decline, periods of relative stability and acute deterioration occur. Historical cohorts often report median survival of two to five years, which is not an individual prediction and predates some current therapies.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33965873 SUPPORT Other
"Although the survival of patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) still hovers around a median of two to five years"
The review summarizes the poor medium-term prognosis that characterizes progressive IPF.
Coexisting conditions affecting evaluation and care
Pulmonary hypertension, emphysema or combined pulmonary fibrosis and emphysema, gastroesophageal reflux, sleep apnea, obesity, coronary artery disease, pulmonary embolism and lung cancer can coexist with IPF. They require clinical consideration without being assumed to arise from one universal IPF mechanism or to explain every deterioration. The cited guideline does not supply disease-specific prevalence estimates or justify universal screening protocols. Treating symptomatic reflux has its own indications; the 2022 guideline conditionally recommends against antacid medication or antireflux surgery solely as IPF treatment.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"Patients with IPF may have sub-clinical or overt co-morbid conditions including pulmonary hypertension, gastroesophageal reflux, obstructive sleep apnea, obesity, and emphysema."
The guideline identifies coexisting conditions without asserting that IPF causes every condition or that treating them reverses fibrosis.
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"Comorbidities including pulmonary hypertension, pulmonary embolism, lung cancer, and coronary artery disease are known to occur in IPF."
The guideline adds vascular and malignant comorbidity context; no universal prevalence, screening benefit or causal arrow is inferred.
PMID:35486072 SUPPORT Other
"Conditional recommendations were made against antacid medication and antireflux surgery for the treatment of IPF."
The current guideline distinguishes IPF-directed treatment claims from management of symptomatic reflux on its own merits.

Differential Diagnoses

3

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis:

Connective tissue disease-associated ILD
Overlapping Features Can produce fibrotic interstitial lung disease with an overlapping UIP pattern.
Distinguishing Features
  • Assess systemic rheumatic manifestations and serology in the clinical context.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"The major differential diagnostic considerations include UIP in other clinical settings such as connective tissue diseases, chronic hypersensitivity pneumonitis (extrinsic allergic alveolitis), and pneumoconioses (especially asbestosis)."
The source identifies major alternative causes of a UIP pattern.
Fibrotic hypersensitivity pneumonitis
Overlapping Features Can produce fibrotic interstitial lung disease with an overlapping UIP pattern.
Distinguishing Features
  • Assess antigen exposure, air trapping and other clinical, imaging and pathologic evidence.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21471066 SUPPORT Other
"The major differential diagnostic considerations include UIP in other clinical settings such as connective tissue diseases, chronic hypersensitivity pneumonitis (extrinsic allergic alveolitis), and pneumoconioses (especially asbestosis)."
The source identifies major alternative causes of a UIP pattern.
Asbestosis
Overlapping Features Can produce fibrotic interstitial lung disease with an overlapping UIP pattern.
Distinguishing Features
  • Assess occupational asbestos exposure and pleural or other compatible imaging findings.

Single-cell RNA-sequencing of normal and fibrotic human lungs (collagen-producing lung cell atlas) scea:E-CURD-126
EBI Single Cell Expression Atlas harmonized re-analysis (102,932 cells) of the Tsukui et al. collagen-producing lung cell atlas, restricted to the human lung subset (normal, idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis, and scleroderma). Resolves collagen-producing mesenchymal heterogeneity and identifies CTHRC1-expressing fibroblasts that appear uniquely in fibrotic lungs and concentrate within fibroblastic foci — cell-resolution support for the activated-mesenchymal-cell and excessive-ECM steps of the fibrotic_response module to which this entry conforms.

Activation of Fibroblast LRP1 by AT2-secreted MDK Affects OTUB1/SLC7A11 Ubiquitination to Inhibit Fibroblast Ferroptosis in Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis. geo:GSE262882
Idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF), a chronic interstitial fibrosing pneumonia of unknown cause, is characterized by an irreversible decrease in alveolar cells and an increase in fibroblasts. In our research, the numbers of AT2 cells and fibroblasts are abnormally regulated, but AT1 cells are significantly reduced in lung tissue sections from patients with IPF and mice. By analyzing the single-cell sequence from GEO database and experimental validation both in vivo and in vitro, it is found that the abnormally regulated AT2 cells secreting a large amount of MDK to activate the CTHRC1+ fibroblasts LRP1 receptor and inhibit the ferroptosis of fibroblasts.

Toll-Like-Receptor 5 Reduces Lung Dysbiosis and Protects Against Pulmonary Fibrosis geo:GSE314583
Idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) is a devastating pulmonary disease with no curative treatment other than lung transplantation. IPF results from maladaptive responses to lung epithelial injury, but the underlying mechanisms remain unclear. Here, we show that deficiency in the innate immune receptor, toll-like receptor 5 (TLR5), is associated with IPF in humans and with increased susceptibility to experimental fibrosis in mice, while activation of lung epithelial TLR5 through a synthetic flagellin analog protects from experimental fibrosis. Mechanistically, epithelial TLR5 activation induces antimicrobial gene expression and ameliorates dysbiosis after lung injury.

Profibrotic priming of airway cell types in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis and drug inhibition in single-cell resolution ega:EGAS00001005794
The cause and etiology of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis, a severe chronic lung disorder with limited treatment options, is still poorly understood. Early genetic studies hinted that airway epithelial cells play an important role in the development of IPF while more recent single-cell RNA sequencing atlases from explant IPF lungs have focused on macrophages. In this study, we used air-liquid interface cultures of primary cells taken from the subsegmental bronchi of IPF patients, reflecting early-stage fibrosis, to interrogate the single-cell transcriptional landscape of the airway mucosa. We identified IPF-enriched cell-cell interaction pathways.

Experimental Models

2
Microengineered alveolar array lung-on-chip with breathing mechanics ORGAN_ON_CHIP namo:OrganOnChip
An array of geometrically defined alveolar units suspended on a thin collagen-elastin hydrogel membrane engineered to match the dimensions and mechanical fragility of the native alveolar basement membrane, with human alveolar epithelial cells and lung fibroblasts co-cultured across it and subjected to controlled cyclic deflection emulating breathing. Precision injection moulding and an SBS microwell format make it medium-throughput, which is what allows drug testing rather than single-device demonstration.
cyclic mechanical stretch (breathing-mimetic) static control TGF-beta 1 stimulation nintedanib treatment

Injection-moulded alveolar array chip with suspended collagen-elastin hydrogel membrane and cyclic actuation
Publication
PMID:41406599
Pathograph links
Fibroblast activation and myofibroblast differentiation recapitulates fidelity: moderate Cyclic stretch applied together with TGF-beta 1 amplified fibrotic signalling well beyond TGF-beta 1 alone. This makes breathing motion an explicit cofactor of fibroblast activation rather than background, which is the mechanistic claim a static culture cannot test.
Limitations: Amplification was demonstrated with exogenous TGF-beta 1 rather than with the endogenous injury signals of IPF, and the co-culture uses non-diseased alveolar epithelial cells and fibroblasts, so it models the mechanical contribution to fibrotic signalling rather than IPF-specific fibroblast biology such as the aberrant basaloid state.
Readouts
Extracellular matrix gene expression under cyclic stretch with TGF-beta 1
Direction: INCREASED
Interpretation: Collagen I, collagen III, and fibronectin induction is the activation readout, and its stretch-dependence is the finding.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41406599 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We demonstrate that cyclic stretching significantly amplifies fibrotic signaling in the presence of transforming growth factor-beta 1 (TGF-β1), evidenced by increased expression of extracellular matrix (ECM) components such as collagen I, collagen III, and fibronectin."
Reports the matrix-gene induction and its dependence on cyclic stretch.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41406599 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Human alveolar epithelial cells and lung fibroblasts are co-cultured on a membrane and subjected to cyclic biomechanical stress that mimics respiratory movements."
Establishes the epithelial-fibroblast co-culture under breathing mechanics as the model configuration relevant to this node.
Excessive extracellular matrix deposition partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate Matrix protein and PAI-1 expression rose under profibrotic stimulation and fell with nintedanib, an approved IPF antifibrotic, giving the model pharmacological validation against the standard of care for this node.
Limitations: Readouts are expression-level over days rather than accumulated cross-linked matrix, and nintedanib reversal establishes assay responsiveness rather than predictive value for novel compounds. The membrane is a defined collagen-elastin hydrogel, not fibrotic human lung matrix, so matrix-stiffness feedback is not reproduced.
Readouts
ECM protein and PAI-1 expression under nintedanib
Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: Reduction by an approved antifibrotic confirms the readout responds in the therapeutically expected direction.

Directly reports the drug-responsive matrix readout.

  relationship: CANONICAL_PATHWAY
  note: >-
    WikiPathways lung fibrosis pathway.
description: >-
  A chronic progressive fibrosing interstitial pneumonia of unknown cause, occurring primarily in older
  adults and associated with the usual interstitial pneumonia (UIP) radiologic and histologic pattern.
  Alveolar epithelial dysfunction, impaired regeneration, fibroblast activation and persistent matrix
  remodeling reduce lung compliance and gas exchange. IPF is diagnosed after exclusion of alternative
  causes of interstitial lung disease; UIP is not specific to IPF. Familial pulmonary fibrosis and telomere
  biology disorders overlap with IPF but also produce other interstitial lung disease diagnoses.
synonyms:
- IPF
- cryptogenic fibrosing alveolitis
- idiopathic fibrosing alveolitis
progression:
- phase: Progressive fibrotic decline
  notes: >-
    Course is heterogeneous: gradual decline, periods of relative stability and acute deterioration occur.
    Historical cohorts often report median survival of two to five years, which is not an individual prediction
    and predates some current therapies.

      Although the survival of patients with idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) still hovers around a
      median of two to five years
    explanation: The review summarizes the poor medium-term prognosis that characterizes progressive IPF.
- phase: Coexisting conditions affecting evaluation and care
  notes: Pulmonary hypertension, emphysema or combined pulmonary fibrosis and emphysema, gastroesophageal reflux, sleep apnea, obesity, coronary artery disease, pulmonary embolism and lung cancer can coexist with IPF. They require clinical consideration without being assumed to arise from one universal IPF mechanism or to explain every deterioration. The cited guideline does not supply disease-specific prevalence estimates or justify universal screening protocols. Treating symptomatic reflux has its own indications; the 2022 guideline conditionally recommends against antacid medication or antireflux surgery solely as IPF treatment.

- name: AT2 cell senescence and SASP
  description: >-
    AT2-lineage cells can enter a stress-associated senescent state with reduced regenerative capacity
    and a secretory program that activates fibroblasts. DNA damage, telomere dysfunction, chronic Wnt
    signaling and metabolic stress are candidate upstream routes. Bleomycin-treated epithelial-fibroblast
    organoids show autocrine TGF-beta feedback and paracrine myofibroblast induction without immune cells.
    In vivo models establish senescence as a possible proximal driver, while the contribution of immune
    recruitment varies by model and cannot be declared universally necessary or dispensable.

- name: Alveolar epithelial telomere dysfunction
  conforms_to: "telomere_attrition#Telomere-Initiated DNA Damage and Replicative Senescence"
  description: >-
    Pathogenic telomere-maintenance variants and age-related shortening can produce dysfunctional telomeres
    and persistent DNA-damage signaling in alveolar epithelial cells. The human genetic evidence supports
    susceptibility, with incomplete penetrance. Experimental shelterin/TRF1 uncapping can induce spontaneous
    lung fibrosis, whereas AT2-specific TERT loss increases bleomycin susceptibility without measurable
    telomere shortening in one model. These perturbations must not be treated as interchangeable demonstrations
    of physiological telomere attrition.

- name: AT2 progenitor exhaustion and impaired alveolar regeneration

- name: Aberrant basaloid cell emergence
  description: >-

- name: Surfactant dysfunction and epithelial proteostasis stress
  description: >-
    Rare surfactant variants predispose a subset of familial interstitial lung disease, sometimes classified

- name: AT2 fatty-acid oxidation impairment
  mechanism_confidence: PROVISIONAL
  description: >-
    FAO gene expression is reduced in human IPF alveolar epithelium. Experimental CPT1a deficiency produces

    explanation: >-

    explanation: >-

- name: Persistent epithelial and mesenchymal TGF-beta signaling

- name: Senescent myofibroblast persistence and apoptosis resistance
  description: >-
    Aging-associated myofibroblasts can fail to dedifferentiate and become resistant to apoptosis. NOX4/NRF2
    redox imbalance, sustained MyoD and BCL-2-dependent survival support persistence in experimental systems.
    Human IPF fibroblasts share aspects of these states. Senescence therefore contributes through mesenchymal
    persistence as well as epithelial dysfunction.
  role: amplifier

      fibrotic lung remodeling.
    explanation: >-
      Conditional anti-apoptotic signaling demonstrates a fibroblast persistence route in mice, not universal
      BCL-2 dependence in humans.

  description: >-

- name: Hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction and vascular remodeling
  description: >-

    vasoconstriction and vascular remodeling contribute to pulmonary hypertension, alongside cytokine

    of fibrosis.

    explanation: This review specifically identifies increased MUC5B expression as part of the molecular program present in fibrotic IPF lung tissue.

  notes: >-

    sometimes classified as IPF, with age-dependent incomplete penetrance. Leukocyte telomere shortening

    telomere length or establish a uniform progression rate.

    explanation: >-

      regardless of underlying diagnosis.
    explanation: >-

      with the mechanistic model of AT2 telomere dysfunction accelerating senescence.

- name: SFTPC
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: SFTPC
    term:
      id: hgnc:10802
      label: SFTPC
  notes: Rare pathogenic variants cause surfactant-related ILD with variable onset and imaging, including some familial cases classified as IPF. Variant-specific trafficking and stress mechanisms differ.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:11991887
    reference_title: Heterozygosity for a surfactant protein C gene mutation associated with usual interstitial pneumonitis and cellular nonspecific interstitial pneumonitis in one kindred.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: We show that an SFTPC mutation segregates with the pulmonary fibrosis phenotype in this kindred
    explanation: Segregation of a heterozygous L188Q variant with familial fibrosis supports a pathogenic SFTPC relationship in a defined kindred; affected adults had UIP and affected children had cellular NSIP. This does not make SFTPC a common cause of sporadic IPF.
  - reference: url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf
    reference_title: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: Pathogenic variants in the surfactant metabolism genes (SFTPA1, SFTPA2, SFTPC) are typically missense variants that lead to increased endoplasmic reticulum stress.
    explanation: GeneReviews identifies pathogenic SFTPC variants as a cause of surfactant-related fibrosis predisposition; the precise mechanism depends on the variant.
  inheritance:
  - name: Autosomal dominant inheritance with variable expression
    inheritance_term:
      preferred_term: Autosomal dominant inheritance
      term:
        id: HP:0000006
        label: Autosomal dominant inheritance
    penetrance: INCOMPLETE
    description: Heterozygous pathogenic SFTPC variants can produce surfactant-related ILD from infancy to adulthood, including adult UIP/fibrosis. Reduced, age-related penetrance and variable expression prevent prediction of an individual carrier’s onset or lung phenotype; the variant-transmission probability is not the disease probability.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:15133475
      reference_title: Nonspecific interstitial pneumonia and usual interstitial pneumonia with mutation in surfactant protein C in familial pulmonary fibrosis.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL

      explanation: The SFTPC L188Q family study directly describes dominant transmission with variable expression across adult UIP and childhood NSIP.
    - reference: url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf
      reference_title: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: OTHER
      snippet: Because predisposition to pulmonary fibrosis is associated with reduced penetrance and intrafamilial clinical variability, the age of onset and clinical manifestations in a sib who inherits a familial pathogenic variant cannot be predicted.
      explanation: The genetic-counseling section supports incomplete penetrance and variable phenotype; Table 1 includes SFTPC among the autosomal dominant surfactant-related causes.
    - reference: url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf
      reference_title: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1230/pdf/Bookshelf_NBK1230.pdf

      explanation: The 50% probability concerns inheritance of a heterozygous pathogenic variant, not a 50% lifetime probability of clinically diagnosed IPF.

    explanation: >-
      The pedigree study supports age-dependent variable disease expression. Pooled gene estimates, missing
      exposure data and competing-risk limitations prevent treating its penetrance numbers as universal
      IPF risks.
